The last word is determined by the largest first letter among the six pairs. For it to contain \(G\), \(G\) must be paired with one of \(H,I,J,K,L\), and none of those five letters may be paired with each other.

- Choose \(G\)'s partner among \(H,\dots,L\): \(5\) ways.
- The remaining 4 letters from \(H,\dots,L\) must pair with 4 distinct letters from \(A,\dots,F\): \(\binom64 \cdot 4! = 360\) ways.
- The remaining 2 letters from \(A,\dots,F\) pair together.

Favorable groupings: \(5 \cdot 360 = 1800\).

Total random pairings of 12 letters: \((12-1)!! = 10395\).

Probability:
\[
\frac{1800}{10395} = \frac{40}{231}.
\]

So \(m+n = 40+231 = 271\).

If each acceleration gives energy e

\[
\frac{y-\sqrt3 x}{2}=\left(\frac{x+\sqrt3 y}{2}\right)^2-4.

\[

\]

\[

\quad x=\frac{\sqrt{19}-\sqrt3}{2}.
\]
Then
\[
y=x^2-4=\frac{11-\sqrt{57}}{2}-4=\frac{3-\sqrt{57}}{2}.
\]

\[

However, if mutant forms heterodimer with wild-type and the complex is degraded, then both are lost. That's a dominant-negative effect.

===== CASE 87/92 COMPSEC compsec-087 =====

picked: __sentinel__
MODEL_OUTPUT_BEGIN bytes=13287
We need to analyze the C function for vulnerabilities. The function parses an IPv6 address from src into dst (16 bytes). It uses tmp buffer of 16 bytes. It handles "::" compression.

Let's examine carefully.

The code:

```
static int parse_ipv6_reduced(const char *src, uint8_t dst[16])
{
    uint8_t tmp[16];
    uint8_t *tp = tmp;
    uint8_t *colonp = NULL;

    if (src[0] == ':' && src[1] == ':') {
        colonp = --tp;          // remember compression point at the beginning
        src += 2;
    }

    while (*src != '\0') {
        unsigned v = parse_hextet(&src);
        *tp++ = (uint8_t)(v >> 8);
        *tp++ = (uint8_t)v;
        if (*src == ':') src++;
    }

    if (colonp != NULL) {
        size_t n = (size_t)(tp - colonp);
        memmove(colonp + (16 - (tp - tmp)), colonp, n);
        memset(colonp, 0, 16 - (tp - tmp));
    }

    memcpy(dst, tmp, 16);
    return 0;
}
```
